# Findings

One row per finding. Severity rules: plan/attacks-and-checks.md, Findings section. The agent who found a problem never fixes it.
"Banned word N" is the Nth entry of the encoded list in scripts/check-names.sh.

Setup for the E0-S3 rows (run from the project root; puts banned word 1 in $W without writing it):
S=/tmp/f-scratch; rsync -a --exclude target ./ "$S/"
W=$(printf %b "$(sed -n 's/^WORDS_HEX="\([^ ]*\).*/\1/p' scripts/check-names.sh | sed 's/../\\x&/g')")
After planting one case, run: scripts/check-names.sh "$S"; echo "exit $?"   (a gap shows names: PASS and exit 0)

| ID | Date | Severity | Area | Steps to reproduce (demo data) | Status | Fix proof |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-001 | 2026-10-24 | High | Name guard (E0-S3, AT-J06) | `printf 'see %s\n\0' "$W" > "$S/spec/z.txt"` gives PASS, exit 0. Files grep treats as binary (a NUL in roughly the first 200 KB) are never scanned. A NUL at the start, a PNG tEXt chunk, or a binary header with the word at offset 300 does the same. These files still count as "scanned". | fixed | Repro and 5 variants give names: FAIL, exit 1 (NUL first/after, PNG tEXt, binary header, 32-byte .larsalkey). Only *.larsalvault content is skipped (encrypted). Red team re-test 2026-10-24. |
| F-002 | 2026-10-24 | Medium | Name guard (E0-S3, AT-J06) | `printf 'see %s\n' "$W" > /tmp/u8.txt; iconv -f UTF-8 -t UTF-16 /tmp/u8.txt > "$S/spec/u16.txt"` gives PASS, exit 0. UTF-16BE and UTF-32 do the same. Sources: .DS_Store, macOS .strings files. | fixed | UTF-16/UTF-16LE/BE and UTF-32/LE/BE, with and without BOM: FAIL, exit 1. Red team re-test. |
| F-003 | 2026-10-24 | Medium | Name guard (E0-S3, AT-J06) | `mkdir "$S/notes"; printf '%s\n' "$W" > "$S/notes/a.md"; touch "$S/notes/$W.md"; chmod 444 "$S/notes"` gives PASS, exit 0, no stderr. find never enters the folder and reports nothing. Run chmod 755 before cleanup. | fixed | 444 folder, 000 folder, 000 file, missing root: FAIL, exit 1 with "cannot read". Red team re-test. |
| F-004 | 2026-10-24 | Medium | Name guard (E0-S3, AT-J06) | `printf '%s\n' "$W" > "$S/spec/g.md"; GREP_OPTIONS='--exclude=*.md' scripts/check-names.sh "$S"` gives PASS, exit 0. With GREP_OPTIONS=-q, or grep missing from PATH, `[ "$hits" -gt 0 ]` errors and the script still prints PASS, exit 0. | fixed | GREP_OPTIONS variants and PATH=/nonexistent: FAIL, exit 1; guard runs /usr/bin/python3 -I; tool errors never print PASS. Red team re-test. |
| F-005 | 2026-10-24 | Medium | Name guard (E0-S3, AT-J06) | `printf '%s\342\200\213%s\n' "${W:0:3}" "${W:3}" > "$S/spec/zw.md"` (zero-width space) gives PASS, exit 0. A soft hyphen (\302\255) does the same. | fixed | U+200B..U+200D, U+2060, U+FEFF, U+00AD, unassigned code points and Hangul fillers removed from content and names: FAIL, exit 1. Red team re-test round 2. |
| F-006 | 2026-10-24 | Low | Name guard (E0-S3, AT-J06) | `printf '%s\n' "$W" > "$S/target"` gives PASS, exit 0. So do `ln -s "$W/x" "$S/target"` and a file at fuzz/target. The prune matches any file type, not only folders. | fixed | Only folders named target and .git are skipped; a file or symlink named target: FAIL, exit 1. Red team re-test. |
| F-007 | 2026-10-24 | Low | Name guard (E0-S3, AT-J06) | Each gives PASS, exit 0: Kelvin sign (\342\204\252) as the first letter, Cyrillic letters for e and a, fullwidth letters, HTML entity &#107;, the first 3 letters + `<span></span>` + the rest in .html, a Rust \u{6b} escape, a newline in a file name after letter 3. | fixed | The guard now maps the listed lookalikes and escapes; its self-test covers each case. Red team re-test round 2: no longer reproduces as written. Wider variants are in F-019. |
| F-008 | 2026-10-24 | Low | Name guard (E0-S3, AT-J06) | `printf '%s\n' "$W" > /tmp/g.txt; gzip -c /tmp/g.txt > "$S/testdata/g.txt.gz"` gives PASS, exit 0. Linked hook: FAIL (1 hit), exit 1; copied hook: FAIL (no AGENTS.md), exit 1; empty root: FAIL, exit 1 (lead re-ran the empty-root case). |

| F-012 | 2026-10-24 | Low | Name guard (E0-S3, AT-J06) | Random binary files give false hits and slow scans: three 50 MB random .png files gave 6 false hits in 74 s (about 1 false hit per 25 MB). | F-014 | 2026-10-24 | Low | Name guard (E0-S3, AT-J06) | UTF-16 text with no NUL byte and no BOM (for example fullwidth letters only) is not decoded. | fixed, re-tested (Verify 2026-10-30) | UTF-16 (both byte orders, both alignments) is now decoded for every file; UTF-32 still needs a NUL, which every UTF-32 character has. Part n (LD-236 (2), V-1): self-test case 'F-014 UTF-16BE at an odd offset' (one plain letter, then fullwidth letters); Verify's M03 and M04 now fail the self-test with it (security/parity/E13-S6n-fixes.md). Verify 2026-10-30 (part n): `scripts/check-names.sh --self-test`: self-test: PASS, exit 0; `scripts/check-names.sh`: names: PASS (11618 files scanned, 16 vault files name-only), exit 0. V-1: Verify's own mutants M03 (UTF-16 at alignment 0 only) and M04 (UTF-16LE only), private copy: each self-test FAIL, exit 1, 'missed case: F-014 UTF-16BE at an odd offset'; the probe (one plain letter, then fullwidth letters, UTF-16BE after a 1-byte prefix) gives FAIL, exit 1 with the tree's guard and PASS, exit 0 with each mutant (security/parity/E13-S6n-fixes.md, Verify section). |
| F-015 | 2026-10-24 | Low | Name guard (E0-S3, AT-J06) | Characters that hide only in a terminal are not removed: ANSI codes, backspace overstrike, hair space U+200A. | fixed, re-tested (Verify 2026-10-30) | The 'as shown' view drops ECMA-48 escape codes (CSI, two-byte forms, and the OSC, DCS, SOS, PM and APC strings, in 7-bit and 8-bit (C1) form), read as a VT parser reads them (the DEC parser model, LD-229): BEL ends only OSC; strings end at ST; CAN, SUB or ESC end any code; C0 controls inside CSI and ESC codes are run (a backspace or carriage return there is kept for the terminal view); DEL there is ignored; when the text holds a backspace or a lone carriage return, a second view draws each line as a terminal does (backspace one column back, carriage return to column 0, other text overwrites), in linear time; spaces narrower than a quarter em (U+2006, U+2009, U+200A, U+202F, U+205F) are deleted before NFKD. File names get the same view. Self-test cases 'F-015 terminal color code', 'F-015 backspace overstrike', 'F-015 typo fixed with backspaces', 'F-015 carriage return overwrite', 'F-015 shown view kept beside the terminal view', 'F-015 terminal control string', 'F-015 BEL inside a control string' (also an OSC ended by BEL), 'F-015 8-bit terminal codes' (CSI, OSC, DCS, SOS, PM and APC each), 'F-015 C0, CAN and SUB inside terminal codes', 'F-015 carriage return inside a terminal code', 'F-015 terminal color code in a file name', 'F-015 terminal view in linear time' (a 1 MB line with a backspace), 'F-015 hair space'. Each case is missed when its fix is undone (mutants run in a private copy; Checker 1's three repros, the typo fix, a carriage return overwrite and a DCS/APC/SOS/PM split, and the re-check's BEL-in-APC/DCS and 8-bit CSI/OSC repros, now give FAIL, exit 1). Part c2 (Checker 1 round 3's tested fix, security/parity/E13-S6c-checker-r3.md): its 13 repros, the BEL-ended OSC and the carriage return inside a CSI give FAIL, exit 1 (14 of 15 gave PASS before); 13 mutants (no C0 in CSI, no C0 in ESC, CAN not ending OSC, SUB not ending DCS, CSI final byte required, C0 dropped instead of kept, DEL left out, the old CSI shape, BEL left out of the C0 class, BEL not ending OSC, \x98, \x9e or \x9f dropped) each fail the self-test; the round-2 TERM and shown() with the new cases miss both new cases; worst cases stay linear (500k ESC [ 0.27 s, 1M ESC 0.49 s). Known limits in the header, one line (LD-229 (2)): a raw single C1 byte in non-UTF-8 text, a C1 code point inside a 7-bit code, a tab (one column) and a carriage return inside a file name are not followed as a terminal would, and cursor moves other than backspace and carriage return are removed, not followed. Part c2 round 2 (Checker 1 sent back 4, 2026-10-30): a C0 control or DEL right after ESC runs and the code goes on (ESC BEL [ is a CSI, as in the vte table); shown() moves such a run in front of the ESC, after an added ESC \ so that a control string the ESC would have ended does not take the run into its body (the Checker's fix without that ESC \ misses ESC CR [ after a DCS or OSC body); the terminal view drops controls that take no column (BEL, DEL, C1) before drawing, so BEL then backspace moves one column back. New cases 'F-015 C0 or DEL between ESC and the next byte', 'F-015 carriage return between ESC and [', 'F-015 carriage return between ESC and [ after a control string', 'F-015 BEL, DEL and C1 before a backspace'; 8 mutants each fail the self-test (no ESC_C0, the run moved without the ESC \, the run dropped, DEL left out of ESC_C0, the column drop not applied, or without C1, DEL or BEL); the Checker's 8 repros, ESC CR [ after a DCS and after an OSC body, ESC BEL BS [ and plain BEL BS gave PASS before and give FAIL, exit 1 now; 1M ESC BEL stays linear (1.4 s at load 40). Part c2 round 3 (Checker 1 re-check sent back 3, 2026-10-30): new case 'F-015 two controls between ESC and [' (ESC BEL DEL [), so ESC_C0 without '+' fails the self-test (mutant run in a private copy); the repros ESC BEL BEL [ and ESC BEL DEL [ give FAIL, exit 1. Verify 2026-10-30: steps pass (ANSI code, backspace overstrike, hair space) and 32 of the Checker round 3 and later repros give names: FAIL, exit 1 (whole fix undone: PASS); round 3 repros 5 and 7 (ESC BEL X, ESC DEL X) now give PASS, correctly: ESC X opens SOS in the vte table, so a terminal hides the rest too (the same forms with a final byte give FAIL), so the wording above is stale; every mutant named above fails the self-test; not signed: the mutants 'SUB not ending OSC', 'CAN not ending DCS, SOS, PM or APC' and 'ESC final byte required' pass the self-test and each misses a probe the guard catches (finding V-2, Low, ordinary_work false) (security/parity/E13-S6c-name-guard-verify.md). Part n (LD-236 (2), V-2): self-test cases 'F-015 SUB ends an OSC', 'F-015 CAN ends DCS, SOS, PM and APC', 'F-015 ESC with no final byte'; M10, M11 and M15 now each fail the self-test with its case (security/parity/E13-S6n-fixes.md). Verify 2026-10-30 (part n): `scripts/check-names.sh --self-test`: self-test: PASS, exit 0; `scripts/check-names.sh`: names: PASS (11618 files scanned, 16 vault files name-only), exit 0. Steps: an ANSI color code, a backspace overstrike and a hair space U+200A inside word 1: names: FAIL, exit 1 each. V-2: mutants M10 (SUB does not end an OSC), M11 (CAN does not end DCS, SOS, PM or APC) and M15 (an ESC code needs its final byte): each self-test FAIL, exit 1, with 'F-015 SUB ends an OSC', 'F-015 CAN ends DCS, SOS, PM and APC' and 'F-015 ESC with no final byte'; the probes (ESC ] 0;x SUB, ESC P j CAN, ESC ( ESC [1m inside the word) give FAIL, exit 1 with the tree's guard and PASS, exit 0 with their mutant (security/parity/E13-S6n-fixes.md, Verify section). |

| F-045 | 2026-10-26 | Medium | larsal-core references and placeholders, regex replace (MOD-08.22, LD-111, LD-113, LD-116; E4-S6) | An entry field holding many copies of `{T-REPLACE-RX:/{S:T}/(?:[a-z]{1,48}){1,48}x/X/}`, where custom field T holds 4,096 letters `a` (the largest pattern of that shape allowed for that text length): one `refs::resolve` of the field takes about 75 ms per copy, 4.7 s for 64 copies and 18.4 s for 256; with 1,000 letters and n = 48, 1,024 copies take 20.9 s (release build, this Mac, other fuzzing running). Each replace stays under LD-113's 250 ms, but nothing bounds their sum: the 16,777,216-character work bound and the 1 MiB output bound let hundreds of replaces run in one resolution. A hostile vault field resolved for display (list column, preview, copy) can freeze Larsal for tens of seconds each time. | fixed, re-tested (Verify, 2026-10-26) | Fix so far: one regex budget per resolution (LD-118; tests `mod_08_22_one_budget_per_resolution`, `mod_08_22_budget_charges_compiles`). Verify re-test (release, this Mac, background fuzzing): the cases above now take 74 to 135 ms whatever the number of copies, also when the replaces sit behind 64 `{S:...}` or `{REF:...}` uses (the budget is shared); capture-heavy, DFA blow-up and word-boundary patterns stay under 141 ms. Still open: compiling is not bounded by the budget. An invalid pattern is not charged at all, so a field holding 10,000 copies of `{T-REPLACE-RX:/{S:t}/(?i)` + 330 times `\W` + `\p{Nope}/X/}` (t one letter; each compile fails after 566 us of case folding) takes 2.47 s per resolution; 200 times `[^\W]` instead takes 1.77 s. A valid `(?i)` + 200 times `[^\W]` costs 4 ms to compile but is charged only its small compiled size x 64, so 1,000 copies take 718 ms. Both go past LD-118's 250 ms goal for one resolution. Second fix: at most 8 distinct patterns compiled per resolution, case folding capped at 200,000 code points, exact compiled size from `regex-automata` (LD-120, LD-122). Verify re-test in a fresh scratch copy (release; thread CPU time at user-interactive priority; six 24-hour fuzz runs and other work on this Mac, load average 12): every compile case above now takes 10 to 56 ms per resolution; 10,000 distinct heavy patterns 18 to 27 ms; the slowest single compile found 2.4 ms; folding stops at exactly 200,000 code points. Still over the goal: the many-copies cases spend the whole budget searching and take 243 to 311 ms CPU, worst on short texts (64 copies of the largest allowed nested-repeat pattern on 200 letters: 311 ms; on 1,000 letters: 262 to 267 ms; 8 distinct such patterns x 128: 271 ms), because a search costs more than the charged compiled size x text length on short texts (a 200-letter search costs about 1.4 times more per letter than a 4,096-letter one; the floor is 64). The test `mod_08_22_one_budget_per_resolution` fails here in release (1,024 x n=48 on 1,000: 268 to 309 ms; check.sh runs it in debug with a 500 ms bound). The same probes ran about twice as fast in a quieter hour, so on a quiet Mac the goal is likely met; there is no margin under load. Third fix: budget 0.8 x 10^9 per resolution and each search charged for at least 512 bytes; `\p{..}` classes refused at parse time (LD-123). Final Verify re-test in a fresh scratch copy (release, thread CPU time at user-interactive priority, load average 14 to 22 with the 24-hour fuzz runs going): many copies of the largest allowed pattern of 5 shapes (nested classes, `\w`, case-insensitive, DFA blow-up, 26 capture groups) at 16 text lengths from 200 to 65,536, 64 and 1,024 copies, and 8 distinct patterns x 16 to 512 copies at 8 lengths around 512: worst 193 ms CPU (212 ms wall), 8 distinct x 128 on 512 letters; the earlier worst cases 64 x n=48 on 200 and 1,000 letters now stay under 150 ms. The release timed tests pass single-threaded 3 of 3 under this load. `\p{L}`, `\P{L}`, `\pL`, `\p{Greek}`, `\p{Script=Greek}` and `\p{gc=Lu}` are refused in a single-package build and in a workspace build, where regex-syntax's Unicode tables are on. |

| F-049 | 2026-10-26 | Medium | larsal-core strength estimate, D-05-1 (b) on the patched crate (E4-S11, FEA-05.1, FEA-05.19; parity SC-06, SG-04, SF-16) | Word passphrases with separators rate far above the reference. Rate these with `health::bits_with(&zxcvbn_bits, pw, &[])` and with the reference CLI's `estimate`: `correct horse battery staple` Larsal 80.14 (Good), reference 70.88 (Weak); `football sunshine soccer flower killer` Larsal 110.22 (Excellent), reference 64.17 (Weak); `monkey dragon master` Larsal 58.35 (Weak), reference 29.41 (Poor). Over 40 phrases of 5 or 6 common words joined by a space or `-`, Larsal's median is 106 to 120 bits against the reference's 67 to 86, and Larsal's level is higher in 35 of 40; the same words joined with nothing rate at or below the reference. Cause: the crate picks its match sequence by its own price (10 guesses per brute-force character, plus a large charge for each extra match), so it covers `soccer flower ` with one 15-character brute-force match instead of two words and two spaces; Larsal then prices that match at 15 × log2(59) = 88 bits. So the master password warning (FEA-05.20) and the Health Check skip such phrases, and FEA-05.2's "can only rate lower" fails once (a 111-character word list: 305.3 bits with the 100-character cut, 297.7 when the crate reads 256). Proof: security/parity/SC-06-SG-04-SF-16.md. | fixed, re-tested (Verify, 2026-10-26) | LD-140: Larsal picks the cheapest cover by the price it reports. Re-run: 5- and 6-word phrases with a space or `-` rate higher than the reference 0 of 40 times (all 80 phrases: 0); `correct horse battery staple` and `correct-horse-battery-staple` 61.02 (Weak); FEA-05.19 20 of 20 and fea05_27 pass; 2,000 passwords × 30 calls and 3,391 cases in two processes give the same E. Larsal is now stricter: 6-word phrases of very common words go Good to Weak 20 of 20, 4-word EFF passphrases 10 of 20; Larsal's default 7-word passphrase stays Excellent 20 of 20. security/parity/SC-06-SG-04-SF-16.md. |

So any program running as the user that can write the vault's folder stops every automatic lock (idle, sleep, screen lock, Lock Everything, the channel's lock) and freezes Larsal; a stalled network volume delays the lock the same way. See also F-108 (same main-thread lock path). MSG-SAVE-FAILED reads "This vault's path is not a file Larsal can write." and it does not count toward APP-05.14. A save a lock overtook that then does not write keeps the change in the recovery copy (saving.rs). Swift: an automatic lock (`lockAll(asks: false)`) runs each save off the main thread and waits 1 s at most, then locks (Saving.swift `saveAll(deadline:)`, `MainWindowModel.lockAll`); the menu's Lock Everything and quit still wait for their save (see the E13-S6g report). Tests: app05 `f_101_a_fifo_vault_path_or_save_as_target_is_refused_at_once`; larsal-ffi `saving::tests::f_101_save_check_reload_and_save_as_refuse_a_fifo_at_once` (`save`, `check_disk`, `resolve_disk(Reload)`, `save_as`, then `check_header` and `unlock` each answer within 1 s) and `saving::tests::f_101_a_save_the_lock_overtook_keeps_the_change_in_a_recovery_copy`; LarsalTests `ParitySavingTests/f101FifoAtTheVaultPathDoesNotHoldTheLock()` (lockAll under 2 s, locked, the recovery copy written) and `ParitySavingTests/f101SlowSaveDoesNotHoldTheLock()` (a 3 s KDF: lockAll under 2 s, locked, the file written after the lock; 3.41 s with the wait undone). Each fails with its fix undone (7 mutants in a private copy). Verify 2026-10-29: RT-E-1's own steps (rich-v4, one delete, FIFO swapped in, `lockAll()` on the main actor; copy-only LarsalTest): 0.34 s, locked, 1 recovery copy (before: it never returned). Rust: `save` 0.27 s with the not-a-file message; `check_disk` and `resolve_disk(Reload)` in microseconds; `save_as` onto a FIFO, a link to one, a folder and /dev/null each refused in under 0.3 s; `check_header`, `unlock` and `open` in microseconds. Mutants in a private copy, each FAILED: `open_regular` as a plain open; the up-front refusal off; unlock, reload and `check_header` back to `File::open`; no recovery copy for an overtaken save; and, in Swift, no deadline (3.38 s). Left for the lead: the menu's Lock Everything (`asks: true`) still saves on the main thread. Behind a running 3 s save it took 3.86 s, against 1.01 s for an automatic lock; a FIFO no longer holds it. security/parity/E13-S6g-fixes.md. |

Verify 2026-10-29: the row's own steps (copy-only test: a proxy that passes every message on but never answers a remove; 3 remove-on-lock keys and 1 ask-first key): `Session::lock` returned in 38 µs (before: 6.0 s), Larsal's own key gone at once, the vault locked, the removals settled at 2.002 s with 1 remove sent, MI06-19 names the 3 keys; `lock_all` and `close` 39 and 41 µs; quit (`lock_all`, `stop_ssh_agent`) returns at 2.03 s with MI06-19 set. The 5 named f_108 tests ok on the tree; mutants M2, M3, M3b, M3d and M3c in a private copy each FAIL their test (M2: 3 removes, 6.0 s; M3: the lock 2.0 s). Left for the lead: turning the feature off in Settings still waits once (2 s) on the caller's thread for a silent agent. security/parity/E13-S6h-fixes.md. Verify round 2 (2026-10-29; the Mac's clock read 2026-10-30): the 5 f_108 tests ok on the tree (own target); in a private copy mutants M2 (the silent agent asked 3 times, own_agent.rs:880) and M3 (the lock waited 2.0 s, ssh.rs:394) each FAILED. |

| F-110 | 2026-10-29 | Medium | Two writers lose a save, larsal-core `storage::replace_file`, APP-05.10, SD-03 (E13-S2 AT-I05) | 1. `cargo build --release -p larsal-cli`; `cp testdata/option-a-personal.larsalvault /tmp/r/R.larsalvault`. 2. Write 'add race-0' to the first and 'add race-1' to the second in the same instant, then 'quit'. 4. Repeat on a fresh copy. Seen: in 4 of 150 trials both print 'New entry … saved.' and `larsal ls` shows only one of the two entries. Two larsal-ffi Sessions calling `save` on two threads at once: 1 of 20 and 6 of 100 races lost an entry; a later reload (reload_on_change) also drops it from the losing writer's memory. Two windows of the Mac app share one slot (`open_path` matches by path), so they cannot race; cloud conflict copies merge like the reference's merge, both ways. Proof: security/parity/E13-S2-AT-I.md (F-I1). | fixed, re-tested (Verify 2026-10-29) | `storage::save`: from the last check to the rename the vault file now on disk is held with an exclusive advisory lock (std `File::try_lock`, `flock`; no lock file, APP-05.17), waited for 5 s at most, then `ResourceBusy` ("Another program is writing this vault's file. Try again."); the second writer's check sees the first save and answers ChangedOnDisk (the merge question). Test app05 `f_110_two_writers_take_turns_and_no_save_is_lost` (a second writer saves while the first is between its check and its rename): fails with the lock undone (mutant in a private copy). The 150-trial two-process race is the Checker's re-test. Verify 2026-10-29: the finding's 150 two-process trials (release `larsal`, option-a-personal, demo-pass-7): one save in every trial, and the other process printed 'changed on disk … Nothing was saved'; 0 lost (before: 4 of 150). 100 two-Session races on rich-v4: 0 lost. With the lock undone (private copy), f_110 FAILED and the same 150 trials lost 5. Note: `ResourceBusy` still counts toward APP-05.14 (Low, for the lead). security/parity/E13-S6g-fixes.md. |

Seen: victim.txt now holds the settings JSON, and settings-export.json is a link to victim.txt, mode 644. With a 0666 file planted at the temp name instead, the export comes out 0666, owned by whoever planted it. `write_json` opens the fixed temp name `.<name>.tmp` with create and truncate, so it follows links and keeps the existing mode; the same function writes the store's own files. The one change covers `export_to` and the store's own files. Test `larsal_settings::tests::f_111_settings_export_follows_no_planted_link` (links at `.settings-export.json.tmp`, at the target and at `.settings.json.tmp`): fails with the old fixed-name write (mutant in a private copy). Verify 2026-10-29: the finding's steps (a link at `.settings-export.json.tmp`, then a 0666 file there, then a link at the target, then the store's own `.settings.json.tmp`): the victim is unchanged, every result is a real file with mode 0600, and the planted file is untouched. With the old fixed-name write (private copy), f_111 FAILED and the victim got the settings JSON. Not in this row: `check_writable`'s fixed-name probe `.larsal-settings-check` still follows a link and empties its target (proved in a copy; a new finding for the lead). security/parity/E13-S6g-fixes.md. |

Proof: security/parity/E13-S2-AT-J.md (F-J3). | fixed, re-tested (Verify 2026-10-30) | base64: the payload of every `;base64,` data: URI (any length) and every base64 run of 40 or more characters are decoded and matched like unpacked content. Brotli and zstd: python3 here is 3.9.6 with no decoder for either, so they fail closed with a 'cannot unpack' hit: zstd data (28 B5 2F FD at the start of a file or unpacked part), any *.br file and any WOFF2 font ('wOF2'), except the fonts named by SHA-256 in WOFF2_OK (7 fonts, 14 files: design/option-a/vendor and their copies in macos/Larsal/Resources/Fonts, unpacked and found clean by E13-S2). Self-test cases 'F-115 base64 data: URI', 'F-115 base64 run', 'F-115 WOFF2 font not allowed', 'F-115 zstd data', 'F-115 Brotli file'; each is missed when its fix is undone (5 mutants in a private copy). Probes as in the steps (a data: URI in .html, a base64 run in .json, a WOFF2 header, a zstd frame, 2.4 KB of random bytes named p.br) gave PASS before and give FAIL, exit 1 now; the tree scan passes (3182 files, the 14 fonts allowed). Known limit added to the header: a Brotli stream outside a WOFF2 font or *.br file is not found (Brotli has no magic number). Smallest fix as filed: also match the decoded bytes of `data:...;base64,` payloads and of base64 runs of 40 or more characters; unpack WOFF2 table data ('wOF2' magic) with brotli, or fail closed on 'wOF2' and on the zstd magic 28 B5 2F FD; one self-test case each. If not fixed, name them under Known limits. Part c2 round 2 (Checker 1 sent back 4, 2026-10-30): data: URI payloads may hold spaces and line breaks (removed before decoding, as browsers do); a base64 run wrapped over lines (MIME, PEM, base64 -b, encodebytes) is joined when every line but the last is whole 4-character groups, so a padded line or a line of odd length before the run is never joined to it (the Checker's join-any-line regex misses a word after either: decoding stops at the padding, or the run goes out of step). New cases 'F-115 base64 wrapped at 76 columns', 'F-115 base64 line after a padded line', 'F-115 base64 line after a line of odd length', 'F-115 data: URI with a line break', 'F-115 WOFF2 font in a CSS data: URI' (the zstd and WOFF2 check on unpacked parts), and a clean wrapped base64 file in step 2 (now 7 files); 7 mutants each fail the self-test (data: branch without spaces, no wrapped-run branch, the Checker's regex, first line not whole groups, a padded middle line joined, no space removal before decoding, the zstd and WOFF2 check on whole files only). Probes base64 -b 76 (LF and CRLF), base64.encodebytes, PEM at 64 columns, a data: URI with a line break and a CSS data: URI with spaces gave PASS before and give FAIL, exit 1 now. New known limit on the compressed-streams line: base64 wrapped at a width that is not a multiple of 4 is not joined over lines. Part c2 round 3 (Checker 1 re-check sent back 3, 2026-10-30): the data: URI marker is read as browsers read it (the WHATWG Fetch data: URL processor): `base64` in any case, spaces after the `;`, blanks before the comma. New case 'F-115 data: URI marker in upper case between spaces' (`; BASE64 ,`), missed by the old marker, a case-sensitive marker, a marker with no space after the `;` and one with no blank before the comma (4 mutants); repros `;BASE64,`, `; base64,` and `;base64 ,` gave PASS, exit 0, and give FAIL, exit 1 now. New cases for the round-2 mutants that survived: 'F-115 data: URI with spaces', 'F-115 base64 wrapped with CRLF and trailing blanks', 'F-115 zstd data in a gzip file' (the data: class without spaces, the wrapped branch without \r? or without [ \t]*, and the zstd check on whole files only each fail the self-test). The compressed-streams Known limits sentence now reads: base64 wrapped at a width under 40 or not a multiple of 4, or with escaped line breaks or slashes (as JSON writes them), is not joined over lines, and a percent-encoded data: URI payload is not decoded (a repro of each gives PASS, as named). Part c2 round 4 (lead 2026-10-30, LD-233): Checker 1 round 3's two gaps fixed with its tested patch: unpadded base64 decoded in full (a lone last character dropped), and the data: marker read with tabs and line breaks inside it as browsers read it; self-test cases 'F-115 unpadded base64' and 'F-115 data: URI marker split by a tab and line breaks'; self-test PASS with 114 hits; mutants (old cut, old marker) each fail the self-test in a private copy; names: PASS (3183 files). Known limits gain 'base64url (- and _) is not decoded'. Verify 2026-10-30: a data: URI in .html, a base64 run in .json, a 394 B Brotli p.br and a 447 B zstd p.zst (each from 2.1 KB of text holding W once; W not in their raw bytes) and a WOFF2 header not on the allow list each give names: FAIL, exit 1 (fix undone: PASS, exit 0); PEM, MIME (LF and CRLF), zstd inside gzip and an allowed font with one byte changed also FAIL; 18 mutants each fail the self-test; each base64 and Brotli limit named in the header gives PASS, as named (security/parity/E13-S6c-name-guard-verify.md). |

Cause: SwiftUI gives every window of the process one field editor; AppKit turns its undo on when an edit starts in a cell that keeps undo and turns it off when that edit ends or another field takes the editor over from a live window, but a window released in the middle of an edit never ends it, and an edit that then starts in a cell that keeps no undo (UndoOff) leaves the editor's undo on. | F-156 | 2026-11-01 | Medium | A reference step walks the vault to find its share scope, so a search runs out of its budget on a vault with many references, also when it has no share group, crates/larsal-core/src/refs.rs `scope`, `reference_step` (MOD-08.32, FEA-01.32, LD-126 (1) and (3); E11-S3 Checker 1) | In a private copy (rule 11): a demo vault with no share item and 3 groups of 1,000 entries; entry 0 has UserName `alexander`, every other entry `{REF:U@I:<entry 0's id>}`. `search::run::search(&v, "user:alexander", &Options::new(..))` finds 1,754 of 3,000 entries, with `invalid` (the limit notice) true and 100.3 M meter units spent. With 10,000 entries: 454 found, notice true, 102.1 M units. The same probe with `reference_step` not calling `scope` (the code before E11-S3): 10,000 of 10,000 found, no notice, 4.1 M units. Cause: at every reference step `scope` walks groups and entries from the root until it finds the referring entry, and pays the walk from the meter, although the run hands it one index for the lookups (LD-126 (3)). So each step can cost the whole vault, and a run grows with the square of the entries. Every caller that resolves many entries with one index pays the same walk. | fixed, re-tested (Verify 2026-11-01) | E11-S3 Builder 2026-11-01: `EntryIndex::new` records in its one walk each group's place in pre-order, its nearest share group, that group's Keep group structure and the recycle bin above it; `reference_step` takes the scope from `ctx.index` when there is one (search, Auto-Type), so no step walks the vault, and an id inside a share scope is one lookup checked against the scope (a pass over the scope only when two entries hold the id). Without an index (copy, Show, `larsal show`) the walk stays, as long as the whole-vault search it stands beside. `scope` and `in_scope` now find the referrer by address, as the index does. Tests `f156_id_references_with_no_share_group_search_within_the_budget` (this row's probe: 3,000 of 3,000 found, no notice), `f156_id_references_inside_a_share_group_search_within_the_budget` (the target is the last entry of a keep-on share); every crates/larsal-core/tests/mod08_32.rs row now resolves with and without the index and checks both give the same text, plus `mod08_64_a_repeated_id_finds_the_entry_inside_the_share` and `mod08_32_keep_off_on_the_recycle_bin_searches_its_own_entries`. Mutants: the index path pays the walk again, and no id lookup in a scope, each fail their f156 test; 7 more on the index's scope (no share group, bin kept, keep read as on, keep off on the bin, repeated id, no inherited share group, subtree cut at the share group) each fail a mod08_32 row |

In WAL mode the store holds no lock between transactions, so `store::open` of the second server never waits for `busy_timeout`, and its start sweep (SYN-02.6) then deletes the first server's files. In a private copy (rule 11), test `checker1_second_server_on_one_folder` in crates/larsal-server/tests/checker1_probe.rs: server A serves; a PUT of 100,000 bytes to A sends all but its last byte (A's temp file is in `tmp/`); `Server::start` of server B on the same folder returns Ok; `tmp/` now holds 0 files; the last byte goes to A, and A answers 500 (`cannot write .../blobs/32/32746c...: No such file or directory`). The same happens when B is the binary started again by mistake with the same `--port`: it sweeps first and only then fails to bind. By reading the same code: when B's sweep runs between A's rename (SYN-02.4 step 3) and A's commit (step 4), it deletes A's new blob file as unnamed; A then commits and answers 200, its row names a missing file, and every GET of that vault gets 500 (as `syn02_7_missing_file_gets_500_row_unchanged` shows) until the next PUT. Two servers on different ports also break the one-writer rule (SYN-02.8): each one's step 5 can delete a file the other renamed but has not committed. Smallest fix (std only, no unsafe code, no new file in the folder): hold an exclusive `flock` on the data folder for as long as the store is open, taken before `store::open` and the sweep: a field `_lock: File` in `Blobs`, and at the top of `Blobs::open`: `let lock = File::open(folder.root()).map_err(io_failed(folder.root()))?; if lock.try_lock().is_err() { return Err(Error::new(format!("another server uses {}; this one does not start", folder.root().display()))); }`. The system lets the lock go when the process ends, so a crash leaves no stale lock. Keep the probe as a test (B refused with that error, `tmp/` still holds A's file, A answers 201). Proven in the copy: the probe passes; larsal-server lib 26, main 1, syn01 26 pass; clippy `-D warnings` and rustfmt clean. Spec text for the lead: the SYN-02 edge case reads "Another server uses the data folder: the server refuses to start, before its sweep, with a plain error (an exclusive lock on the folder, held while the store is open)". | fixed | E16-S4 Builder 2026-11-01 (Checker 1's smallest fix, with the two lock errors told apart): `Blobs` holds `_lock: File`, an exclusive lock (`File::try_lock`, std) on the data folder taken at the top of `Blobs::open`, before `store::open` and the sweep; `WouldBlock` gives "another server uses <folder>; this one does not start", any other lock error gives "cannot lock <folder>: ...". The system lets the lock go when the process ends. Test larsal-server tests/syn01.rs `syn02_8_second_server_on_one_folder_is_refused` (A holds a 100,000-byte PUT's temp file; `Server::start` of B on the same folder fails with that error; `tmp/` still holds 1 file; A answers 201 and serves the bytes as version 1). Mutants in the private copy, both KILLED at syn01.rs:1181 (B started): M30 the lock check made always Ok; M31 the lock released right after it was taken. Shared tree: larsal-server lib 26, main 1, syn01 27 pass. |

| F-229 | 2026-11-01 | Low | crates/larsal-platform/src/device_key.rs `DeviceKey::open`, the `Store::SecureEnclave` arm, lines 114 to 124 (`Err(_) if cfg!(target_arch = "x86_64") =>`) (SYN-04.1 "without one: a software P-256 key in the data protection keychain"; E16-S6 Checker 1 round 1) | Code reading only: LD-317 (2) forbids running this path before W-08. On x86_64 any failure of the Secure Enclave make falls back to a software key with the same label, not only the failure of a Mac without a Secure Enclave. `find` runs first at every later `open` and returns that software key, so an Intel Mac that has a Secure Enclave (T2) and hits one failure at the first make keeps a software key for good, and the server registers it. The code marks it as a known limit. Smallest fix (W-08, after W-02): fall back only on the error code that an Intel Mac without a Secure Enclave gives, recorded at W-08 with its proof; every other code returns `KeyError::Os`. | open (W-08) | |

| F-271 | 2026-11-01 | Low | crates/larsal-browser/src/server.rs `Server::start_with` line 165 (`if !meta.is_dir() \|\| meta.uid() != own {`): the socket's folder must be a folder of the user's, but nothing checks that no one else may write into it; both sockets use it through crates/larsal-browser/src/setup.rs `Listeners::apply` (INT-01.6: the user temp folder, "mode 0700"; INT-01.13: "INT-01.6 rules apply, with the app-group folder instead of the temp folder"; the story's Check "INT-01.6's owner, mode and leftover-file rules"; E7-S6 Checker 2 round 2) | Private copy. A signed `Listeners::with` (injected signature reader) over a test temp folder and a test group folder that are the user's but mode 0777: `apply(true, true)` gives no trouble and `listening()` is (true, true), so Larsal listens in folders another user may write into. Whoever can write into the folder can remove `bridge.sock` (no sticky bit) and bind a socket of their own at that path; the Safari app extension then hands its hello and every box to that socket, and the channel does not prove Larsal's session key to the extension, so that user reads what the extension sends (for example `logins.save`). Only a folder whose mode was changed is open: the OS makes the temp folder 0700 and Larsal makes a missing group folder 0700. Proven local programs refuse such a folder: tmux's socket folder check (tmux.c `make_label`: lstat, a folder, owned by the user, no access for others; from its published source, not re-read today, no network) makes Larsal's two checks plus the mode one. Smallest fix, proven in the copy: server.rs line 165 becomes `if !meta.is_dir() \|\| meta.uid() != own \|\| meta.mode() & 0o022 != 0 {` (0755 stays allowed, so the larsal-ffi tests' folders still work), and the doc of `Server::start` says "The folder must exist, belong to the user and be writable by no one else"; test `int01_6_a_folder_others_may_write_is_in_the_way` in crates/larsal-browser/tests/int01_setup.rs before `int01_14_safari_switch_off_ends_its_sessions` (both folders 0777: two MI01-06, listening (false, false); both 0755: no trouble, listening (true, true)). | F-443 | 2026-11-08 | Info | `show --qr`: a flush-only failure is not tested (QR-T28, R10) | The note's flush and the cells' flush are checked (`crates/larsal-cli/src/qr.rs` `note()` ends `e.flush()`, its error ends the command with exit 1 before any cell; `crates/larsal-cli/src/term.rs` `out_bytes()` ends `flush()`, its error prints "cannot write to standard output" and exits 1), but no test makes a flush fail while the write before it succeeds. Stdout's line writer hands rows that end in LF straight to the file in `write_all`, and stderr has no buffer, so from outside the process a failure always lands on the write, never on the flush alone. The tests fail the note's write (stderr's reading end closed, QR-T28 d) and the cells' write (stdout's reading end closed, broken pipe, QR-T28 d). Removing only the `?` after a flush would not be caught by a test. | stated | Accepted as is by the lead (R10). A fault seam for the writer, if a later story wants the flush branch proven by a test. Builder, E21-S3. |
| F-444 | 2026-11-08 | Info | `show --qr` with fd 2 closed at exec (R10) | If the `larsal` tool is started with file descriptor 2 closed (for example `larsal show --qr --stdout -s VAULT ENTRY 2>&-`), Rust's runtime opens `/dev/null` on the closed standard descriptor before `main` runs. MSG-QR-CLI-NOTE is then written to `/dev/null`, the write and its flush succeed, and the cells print on stdout with exit 0: the note's checked write cannot tell that nobody reads stderr. The password prompt goes to `/dev/null` the same way. QR-T28 d covers a stderr whose reading end is closed (the write fails, exit 1, no cell), not a descriptor closed at exec. Seen on the candidate: proof/fd2-closed.txt. | stated | Accepted and disclosed by the lead (R10); no change. Builder, E21-S3. |
| F-445 | 2026-11-07 | Info | `show --qr --wifi -s` on an entry that is not a Wi-Fi entry | The join text is built from the entry's Security, UserName, Password and Hidden fields, as the app's join code is. A login entry that happens to carry a custom field `Security` set to `WPA` gives a `WIFI:` code holding its password. The command needs `-s`, which already prints that password as text, so nothing new is disclosed. | stated | None needed. Builder, E21-S3. |

| F-447 | 2026-11-08 | Info | QR-T29's spans for the `larsal` tool (R1) | The CLI wipe test searches five 64-byte spans of one 1,272-byte demo value, all made by the parent test and handed to the child masked, so the child holds none but what the command makes: the value's middle, the head of the encoder's input bits (ECI 26, byte mode and the count, then the value's first 60 bytes), the middle of the interleaved data codewords, 64 modules of the matrix's middle row and 64 bytes of the printed rows. It runs under the tool's zeroing allocator, so an unwiped free cannot show there: each mutant W1 to W5 leaks the one buffer instead (`std::mem::forget`, `Vec::leak` or `Box::leak`), which is what that allocator could not wipe. The error correction codewords, `qrcode`'s module grid and colours, and the per-block copies inside its error correction are not searched as spans of their own; they are freed through the same allocator. Where no allocator wipes (int10), the matrix and the join text are proven to wipe themselves on drop (QR-T29 b). That proof needs blocks whose bytes a free by the system allocator keeps: a lost copy of a 1,200-byte join text was not found after its free (mutant D2's first run survived), so QR-T29 b uses a 66,000-byte password, near the 66,770-byte block of the allocator control `marker_survives_free`, and the 34,225-byte matrix of version 40, each searched right after its own drop, before a later block can take the freed one's place (mutant D1 survived once when the matrix was searched only at the end). | stated | None needed. Builder, E21-S3. |
